PQC Today capability coverage matrix

PQC Today executes selected public reference vectors, standards tests, conformance cases, and implementation probes. A passing result is evidence only for the identified test, operation, parameters, implementation build, and target. It is not an ACVTS verdict, a CAVP/CMVP certificate, or proof of exhaustive conformance.

Generated from the runtime mechanism inventory, the reviewed capability map and the registered test inventory. A status says a registered test exists for the cell; it is not a pass. Machine-readable copy: coverage-matrix.json.

Headline

  • C++: 1635 advertised capability cells (165 mechanisms). Positive: 98 covered, 124 sampled, 1413 untested. Negative: 35 covered, 25 sampled, 1575 untested. 27 further cells unsupported. WebAssembly runs: 197 cells with a recorded pass, 8 with a recorded fail, 0 with a recorded skip (not run, never a pass).
  • Rust: 1025 advertised capability cells (172 mechanisms). Positive: 102 covered, 127 sampled, 796 untested. Negative: 35 covered, 25 sampled, 965 untested. 637 further cells unsupported. WebAssembly runs: 214 cells with a recorded pass, 13 with a recorded fail, 0 with a recorded skip (not run, never a pass).

Rules and definitions

cell
A capability cell is one (mechanism, operation, parameter set, sign variant). Mechanism, operation (from CKF_ flags) and key-size range are DISCOVERED from the running engine (C_GetMechanismList + C_GetMechanismInfo); parameter sets, sign variants and boundaries are DECLARED in capability-map.json and kept only when the parameter set falls inside the engine-reported key-size range.
denominator
Denominator (per engine) = advertised capability cells: the engine lists the mechanism, sets the CKF_ flag for the operation, and the declared parameter set falls inside its reported key-size range. Cells the engine does not advertise, and declared capabilities no PKCS #11 mechanism can express, are counted separately as unsupported and shown — never dropped.
status
Cell status = the strongest evidence class among registered test cases that exercise the cell on that engine (order: acvts-issued > nist-reference > standard-kat > oracle > differential > round-trip > behavior-only). No registered case = untested. Not advertised = unsupported. A status says a test EXISTS; it is not a pass.
covered
Covered (per polarity) = at least 2 distinct registered cases of that polarity exercise the cell AND, for positive, at least one of them checks an externally expected value (acvts-issued, nist-reference, standard-kat or oracle). A cell is covered overall only when positive, negative, boundary and state-error are all covered.
sampled
Sampled = at least one registered case, but the covered rule is not met. One happy-path case is always "sampled", never "covered" (plan Q5).
polarity
Polarities are counted separately: a positive case never counts toward negative, boundary or state-error. Boundary = a registered case whose recorded parameters hit a declared boundary of the cell (e.g. context length 0 or 255, empty message); it may also be a positive or negative case. State-error = a case asserting a PKCS #11 state or argument error.
parity
Parity = both engines have a RECORDED PASS of the same registered case on the same artifact kind. Skipped, unsupported and not-run never count as a pass, and a recorded result counts only for the exact artifact (sha256) the mechanism inventory records. Rows with no such pair are "not established".
artifacts
Artifact kinds are reported separately: wasm (the two shipped WebAssembly engines — registered tests run in the browser), native, hardware. Native and hardware targets have not been run: they are "not run" and never counted as pass.
inventory
Per engine and artifact kind the matrix counts, separately: advertised cells (the denominator — C_GetMechanismList × operation × parameter set × variant), registered cells (≥ 1 registered case, by evidence status), cells with a recorded pass, with a recorded fail, and with a recorded skip (the engine did not advertise a mechanism the case needs, so it was not run), unsupported cells (not advertised) and untested cells (advertised, no registered case). A skip is never a pass and is never folded into untested.
numerator
Numerators (per engine, per polarity) = advertised cells whose level is covered, sampled or untested under the rules below; covered + sampled + untested = the denominator. Per-status numerators count advertised cells by their strongest registered evidence class.

Artifact kinds and parity

  • wasm — registered; partly recorded. Registered tests target the shipped WebAssembly engines. Recorded results: 94 from katRunner (every registered KatKind) (Node.js via vitest local venue (not a browser), 2026-09-25, hub 88609829b); 1356 from useAcvpSuite (all categories, dual engine) (Node.js via vitest local venue (not a browser), 2026-09-25, hub ea5aa8f64). Tests without a recorded result are never reported as passed.
  • native — not-run. macOS, Linux x86-64 and Linux Arm64 native builds have not been run through these tests (plan H-2). Never counted as pass.
  • hardware — not-run. i.MX 95 and KV260 targets have not been run through these tests (plan H-3). Never counted as pass.

Shown in the workbench as an explicit skip (mldsa-skip-hedged-rnd-*) and here as unsupported rows.
HashML-DSA with SHA2-512/224 or SHA2-512/256 has no PKCS #11 mechanismaccepted-limitationunassignedD2-6C++ + Rust (specification limit)—PKCS #11 v3.2 §6.67.7 defines no CKM_HASH_ML_DSA_<hash> for these two hashes; the NIST sigVer groups for them are listed as notExecuted in mldsa_sigver_test.json.
ML-DSA internal interface (raw M′) is not expressible through PKCS #11accepted-limitationunassignedD2-5C++ + Rust (specification limit)—No PKCS #11 v3.2 mechanism exposes Sign_internal/Verify_internal on a caller-supplied M′; the NIST internal-interface groups without external mu are listed as notExecuted.

Treated as findings, not verdicts; cells one engine does not advertise are unsupported for that engine.
7 mechanisms advertised by Rust onlyopenunassignedG-9C++—CKM_RIPEMD160, CKM_RIPEMD160_HMAC, CKM_RIPEMD160_HMAC_GENERAL, CKM_PQCTODAY_FRODOKEM_KEY_PAIR_GEN, CKM_PQCTODAY_FRODOKEM_ENCAPSULATE, CKM_KECCAK_256 and CKM_EC_MONTGOMERY_KEY_DERIVE are listed by the Rust engine only; their C++ cells are unsupported.

Also dispatched-not-advertised: Rust CKM_BIP32_*_LEGACY and C++ CKM_RIPEMD160*.

Run results recorded only for the workbench and katRunner, on the wasm engines, in NodeopenunassignedC-4 / H-1 / H-7C++ + Rust (wasm)—src/data/validation/run-results/ holds one recorded run of useAcvpSuite (all categories, both engines) and one of every registered katRunner case (both engines asserted, the Rust engine recorded — the engine the KAT surfaces use), executed in Node against the shipped .wasm bytes. The mechanism probes and the conformance cases have no recorded results, so their cells are never reported as passed. A browser-host run and a signed evidence bundle (H-1, H-7) do not exist yet.

SLH-DSA NIST sigGen cases registered but not executedopenunassignedD3-4C++ + Rust—slhdsa_ctx_test.json /sigGen cases are in the manifest for identity but no runner executes them (deterministic sigGen byte-match), so SLH-DSA sign has round-trip evidence only.
Supported ACVP test types (AFT/MCT/LDT/VAL) not declared per algorithmopenunassignedC-1all—The capability map declares parameter sets, variants and boundaries, but not which ACVP test types each capability should support; the matrix lists only the test types its registered NIST cases exercise.
Some test surfaces are not in the test registryopenunassignedC-2C++ + Rust—Not registered: Learn-module demos outside KatValidationPanel (the SUCI flow's own step-by-step tool among them) and the *.local.test.ts engine suites that are not run-results recorders. Their evidence does not appear in this matrix. (The katRunner suci-profile-b chain is registered since 2026-09-24: steps 3-7 against TS 33.501 Annex C.4.4.1.)
Rust engine accepts all 6 invalid NIST ML-KEM encapsulation/decapsulation keysopenunassignedD1-5Rust (wasm)—The NIST ACVP ML-KEM encapDecap VAL key-check cases (mlkem_encapdecap_val_test: modified H, ek values too large) are accepted by the Rust engine: its C_EncapsulateKey / C_DecapsulateKey paths perform no FIPS 203 §7.2 encapsulation-key or §7.3 decapsulation-key input checks (bundle 417c47a2; unchanged on pqctoday-hsm origin/main). The workbench rows stay failing on Rust (acvp.07b.keycheck). Engine defect — not patched in the hub.
C++ HashSLH-DSA wraps M′ twice — valid NIST pre-hash signatures rejectedopenunassignedD3-3C++ (wasm)—OSSLSLHDSA.cpp (bundle 7795799b, lines ~341-402) builds M′ = 0x01 ‖ len(ctx) ‖ ctx ‖ OID ‖ PH(M) itself and then passes it to OpenSSL's pure SLH-DSA via EVP_DigestSign together with OSSL_SIGNATURE_PARAM_CONTEXT_STRING, so OpenSSL wraps it again as a pure message: all 5 valid NIST pre-hash sigVer signatures are rejected and deterministic pre-hash signatures differ at byte 0. Unchanged on pqctoday-hsm origin/main. Rows stay failing on C++ (acvp.09c). Engine defect — not patched in the hub.
ML-KEM wrong-length inputs: return codes differ from PKCS #11 v3.2 and between enginesopenunassignedD1-5 / G-8C++ + Rust (wasm)—Wrong-length ciphertext at C_DecapsulateKey: Rust returns CKR_ENCRYPTED_DATA_INVALID (not in the return-value list of PKCS #11 v3.2 §5.18.9), C++ returns CKR_WRAPPED_KEY_LEN_RANGE. The C++ engine accepts a decapsulation key 1 byte short at C_CreateObject; both engines accept a short encapsulation key. The boundary rows (acvp.07b.boundary) pin each engine's observed code, so a pass there means 'behaves as pinned', not 'conforms'.
PBKDF2 iteration floor differs between engines (Rust refuses c < 1000)openunassignedG-9C++ vs Rust (wasm)—The Rust engine refuses CKM_PKCS5_PBKD2 with iterations < 1000 (rust/src/ffi.rs:11125 at 417c47a2: CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD, a code that does not name the parameter); the C++ engine accepts c = 1 (SoftHSM_keygen.cpp ~3012-3030 at 7795799b). A policy divergence, not an algorithm error. The c = 1 oracle cases (pbkdf2_test tests/0) are therefore not registered; katRunner and the workbench run the c = 4096 cases, which pass on both engines.
ECDSA P-521 NIST sample failed on C++ — hub CKA_EC_POINT encoding (fixed hub-side)in-progressunassignedE (ECDSA) / J-7C++ (wasm) — hub adapter—Root cause (triage 2026-09-24): hsm_importECPublicKey wrote the DER OCTET STRING length of the 133-byte P-521 point as the single byte 0x85, which DER reads as '5 length octets follow'; the C++ engine parsed a garbage CKA_EC_POINT and rejected the NIST ACVP-Server ECDSA-SigVer-FIPS186-5 sample (workbench §33), while Rust passed because it reads the raw CKA_VALUE copy. Hub adapter bug, not an engine defect. Fixed by the X.690 long-form length (softhsm/helpers derOctetString); the re-recorded run shows the case passing on both engines. Kept on record until the release freeze re-captures evidence on the browser host.
SUCI key-injection helper is rejected by the C++ engineopenunassignedA-3C++ (wasm) — hub helper—hsm_injectTestKey unwraps the raw EC scalar with C_UnwrapKeyAuthenticated using a template that carries CKA_EC_PARAMS; the C++ engine rejects it with CKR_ATTRIBUTE_READ_ONLY (the same unwrap without CKA_EC_PARAMS succeeds). katRunner suci-profile-b steps 1-2 (import only, not registered) therefore error on C++; steps 3-7 import with C_CreateObject and pass on both engines. The KAT surfaces run the Rust engine. Hub helper limitation.
Rust engine refuses AES-GCM IVs other than 96 bitsopenunassignedE (AES-GCM) / G-9Rust (wasm)—The NIST ACVP-Server ACVP-AES-GCM-1.0 sample (aesgcm_acvp_test, workbench §1b) has two groups with a 120-bit IV (tg2 encrypt, tg4 decrypt). The Rust engine refuses them at C_EncryptInit/C_DecryptInit with CKR_MECHANISM_PARAM_INVALID (rust/src/ffi.rs ~8158 at 7795799b: 'AES-GCM requires a 12-byte nonce'); SP 800-38D and PKCS#11 v3.2 §6.13.2 allow other IV lengths and the C++ engine passes all 60 cases. The 30 Rust rows fail, including tg4's upstream authentication failures, which are refused before the tag is checked and therefore do not count as rejections. Engine limitation or advertisement to narrow — a decision for the engine owner.
Rust engine accepts P-224 keys but cannot verify P-224 ECDSA signaturesopenunassignedE (ECDSA) / G-7 / G-9Rust (wasm)—ECDSA-SigVer-FIPS186-5 P-224 groups (ecdsa_sigver_acvp_test, workbench §4b): C_CreateObject accepts the P-224 public key, then every C_Verify returns CKR_SIGNATURE_LEN_RANGE for the 56-byte r||s — the Rust crate set has no P-224 (no p224 dependency at 7795799b) although CKM_ECDSA* advertise ulMinKeySize 112. All 28 Rust P-224 rows fail (valid cases rejected; invalid cases refused for the wrong reason, which the section does not count as a rejection). C++ passes all 112 ECDSA cases. P-224 is also outside the capability map's EC curve set, so these cases map to no capability cell; their results are recorded.
HMAC keys below the advertised ulMinKeySize are accepted by both enginesopenunassignedG-1 / G-7C++ + Rust (wasm) — advertisement—C_GetMechanismInfo advertises ulMinKeySize = the digest length in bytes for every CKM_<hash>_HMAC[_GENERAL] (e.g. 32 for SHA2-256), yet both engines compute and verify NIST HMAC 2.0 cases with shorter keys (down to 1 byte) correctly (hmac_acvp_matrix_test, workbench §2b; each row records keyInAdvertisedRange). FIPS 198-1 allows any key length, so the results are correct; the advertisement understates what the engines accept. Recorded, not enforced.
Classical NIST reference samples cover only what the pinned upstream samples registeropenunassignedEC++ + Rust—The pinned ACVP-Server@975de31e samples bound what WS-E can claim: ACVP-AES-GCM-1.0 registers AES-128 only (IV 96/120, tag 128/32, payload/AAD 0/120 bits) — AES-192/256-GCM, 64-bit tags and other lengths have no NIST case; HMAC 2.0 registers macLen 80..160 bits — no full-length (untruncated) tag, so CKM_<hash>_HMAC (non-general) has no NIST case; EDDSA-SigVer-1.0 carries no context string (Ed25519ctx / Ed448 contexts not claimed); ECDSA SHA2-512/256 and SHAKE groups have no PKCS#11 v3.2 CKM_ECDSA_<hash> mechanism (skip rows).
Rust engine answers a wrong-length AES-CBC IV with CKR_ARGUMENTS_BADopenunassignedG-8 / G-9Rust (wasm)—Product-authored probe aescbc-probe-iv15 (workbench §12b): C_EncryptInit(CKM_AES_CBC) with a 15-byte IV. The C++ engine returns CKR_MECHANISM_PARAM_INVALID; the Rust engine returns CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD, which PKCS#11 v3.2 §5.8.1 does not list for C_EncryptInit (CKR_MECHANISM_PARAM_INVALID is the listed code for a bad mechanism parameter). The Rust row stays red; the probe pins the spec code for both engines.
Rust engine cannot verify RSA signatures whose public exponent exceeds 2^33 - 1openunassignedE (RSA) / G-9Rust (wasm)—RSA-SigVer-FIPS186-5 (rsa_sigver_acvp_test, workbench §4d): the upstream 4096-bit PKCS#1 v1.5 group (e = 0xC9986A9C84FEE9, 56 bits) and the 2048-bit PSS/SHA3-256 group (e = 0x0ACC245201D531, 52 bits) import, but every C_Verify returns CKR_KEY_TYPE_INCONSISTENT on the Rust engine — rsa::RsaPublicKey::new (rust/src/crypto/handlers.rs ~2895 at 7795799b) refuses exponents above 2^33 - 1. FIPS 186-5 §A.1.1 allows 2^16 < e < 2^256. 12 Rust rows fail (valid cases rejected, invalid ones refused for the wrong reason); C++ passes all 24. The groups were chosen by the deterministic subset policy (first group per combination), not by result.
Rust engine's CKM_PKCS5_PBKD2 implements only the HMAC-SHA-256/384/512 PRFsopenunassignedE (PBKDF2) / G-7 / G-9Rust (wasm)—PBKDF 1.0 (pbkdf2_acvp_test, workbench §18b): the pinned NIST sample registers HMAC-SHA2-224 only. The Rust engine answers CKP_PKCS5_PBKD2_HMAC_SHA224 with CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD (rust/src/ffi.rs ~11138 at 7795799b: prf match arms SHA256/384/512 only, every other PRF -> CKR_ARGUMENTS_BAD, a code that does not name the parameter); CKM_PKCS5_PBKD2 is advertised without any PRF restriction. All 5 Rust rows fail; C++ passes all 5, including the 1-iteration case that the Rust iteration floor (pbkdf2-min-iterations-divergence) would refuse anyway.
KMAC C_Verify accepts only the default MAC length, whatever ulOutputLen saysopenunassignedE (KMAC) / G-9C++ + Rust (wasm) — vendor mechanism—KMAC-128 1.0 MVT (kmac_acvp_test, workbench §35b): the valid case tc799 has a 478-byte MAC; both engines accept CK_PQCTODAY_KMAC_PARAMS.ulOutputLen = 478 at C_VerifyInit but C_Verify returns CKR_SIGNATURE_LEN_RANGE because the signature length is checked against the mechanism's fixed default (32 bytes for KMAC-128; rust/src/ffi.rs ~7295-7336 at 7795799b lists CKM_KMAC_128/256 among the fixed-length MACs). The invalid case tc771 (a 32-byte MAC) is rejected with CKR_SIGNATURE_INVALID on both. The pinned KMAC-256 sample has no byte-aligned non-XOF case, so KMAC-256 still has no NIST case (the older kmac_test KMAC256 case stays quarantined). Vendor mechanism, both engines.
Classical families still without NIST ACVP-Server cases after WS-EopenunassignedEC++ + Rust—Not yet sourced from the pinned ACVP-Server samples (remediation plan WS-E, P1 breadth): HKDF (KDA-HKDF-Sp800-56Cr2 needs fixedInfo assembly), SP 800-108 KBKDF (KDF-1.0 counter/feedback), ANSI X9.63 KDF (kdf-components-ansix9.63: PKCS#11 exposes it only inside CKM_ECDH1_DERIVE, which takes no caller-supplied Z, so the upstream (Z, sharedInfo) -> key mapping cannot be driven), ECDSA/EdDSA KeyVer (C_CreateObject is not a key-validation operation) and ECDSA/EdDSA SigGen verify-back, the alternate-version SHA-2 MCT (SHA2-256/512/512-256), full 100-iteration MCTs (only outer iteration 0 is checked for SHA-2/SHA-3 standard MCT and AES-CBC MCT), and SHA LDT. The existing HKDF/PBKDF2/X9.63/KBKDF workbench rows keep their published-standard / oracle / round-trip labels.
